Don’t start reinstalling or upgrading anything on the first pass.\\n\\nStart with **System 1** and use this exact sequence.\\n\\n1. **Identify the machine**\\n   - User/department\\n   - Computer name\\n   - Laptop/Desktop\\n   - Brand/model\\n   - Serial number/service tag\\n\\n2. **Check hardware**\\n   - Processor\\n   - RAM\\n   - HDD or SSD\\n   - Total disk size\\n   - Free space on C:\\n   - 32-bit / 64-bit\\n\\n   On Windows, press:\\n   **Win + R → `msinfo32`**\\n\\n   Then:\\n   **Win + R → `diskmgmt.msc`**\\n\\n3. **Check Windows**\\n   Press:\\n   **Win + R → `winver`**\\n\\n   Record:\\n   - Windows 7/10/11/Server\\n   - Edition\\n   - Version/build\\n   - Activation status\\n\\n   For activation:\\n   **Settings → Activation**\\n   or run:\\n   **Win + R → `slmgr.vbs /xpr`**\\n\\n4. **Check all drives and data locations**\\n   \\n   Before changing anything, identify where their important customer data actually sits:\\n   - Desktop\\n   - Documents\\n   - Downloads\\n   - C:\\\\ custom folders\\n   - D:\\\\ / E:\\\\ drives\\n   - Tally/ERP folders\\n   - Shared/network drives\\n   - OneDrive if used\\n   - PST/Outlook data if used\\n\\n   At this stage **do not move or reorganize anything yet**. Just record it.\\n\\n5. **Check Microsoft Office**\\n   \\n   Open Word → **File → Account**\\n\\n   Record:\\n   - Office version\\n   - Microsoft 365 / Office 2016/2019/2021/etc.\\n   - Activation status\\n   - Signed-in account, if any\\n   - 32/64-bit if visible\\n\\n   Then test Word with a brand-new test file:\\n   - Open\\n   - Type\\n   - Save\\n   - Close\\n   - Reopen\\n   - Edit\\n   - Save again\\n\\n   Repeat with Excel.\\n\\n6. **Record the exact Office problem**\\n\\n   Don't write only “Word issue.”\\n\\n   Write things like:\\n   - Word opens but editing disabled\\n   - Unlicensed Product\\n   - Activation required\\n   - Excel crashes on startup\\n   - Existing XLSX opens read-only\\n   - DOCX file doesn't open\\n   - Word works online but desktop app doesn't\\n   - Save fails\\n   - Office app missing completely\\n\\n7. **Check installed applications**\\n   \\n   Open:\\n\\n   **Win + R → `appwiz.cpl`**\\n\\n   Record important software such as:\\n   - Microsoft Office\\n   - Tally\\n   - ERP/accounting applications\\n   - Adobe/PDF software\\n   - Java\\n   - Chrome/Edge\\n   - Antivirus\\n   - Any industry-specific software\\n\\n   Don't worry about recording every Windows component.\\n\\n8. **Check Device Manager**\\n   \\n   **Win + R → `devmgmt.msc`**\\n\\n   Look for yellow warning icons.\\n\\n9. **Check Windows Update**\\n   \\n   Record:\\n   - Fully updated\\n   - Updates pending\\n   - Update error\\n   - Unsupported OS\\n\\n   Don't trigger huge upgrades yet.\\n\\n10. **Check security**\\n   - Antivirus installed?\\n   - Antivirus running?\\n   - Windows Defender enabled?\\n   - Unknown antivirus?\\n   - Any suspicious/cracked software?\\n   - Local admin users?\\n\\n11. **Check basic system health**\\n   - PC very slow?\\n   - Startup slow?\\n   - Disk constantly at 100%?\\n   - Low disk space?\\n   - Random restart?\\n   - Application crashes?\\n   - Internet working?\\n   - LAN/network shares working?\\n\\n12. **Data risk classification**\\n\\n   Mark each PC:\\n\\n   **DATA-HIGH**\\n   Customer/business data stored locally and no confirmed backup.\\n\\n   **DATA-MEDIUM**\\n   Local data exists but backup/shared copy available.\\n\\n   **DATA-LOW**\\n   Mostly applications; files stored centrally/cloud/server.\\n\\nThis one classification is very important because a PC with **DATA-HIGH** should never be your first candidate for experimentation or OS reinstall.\\n\\nFor each system, keep one record like:\\n\\n| Item | System 1 |\\n|---|---|\\n| PC Name | NB-CLIENT-PC01 |\\n| User | Accounts |\\n| CPU | Intel i5 |\\n| RAM | 8 GB |\\n| Storage | 1 TB HDD |\\n| C Free Space | 32 GB |\\n| Windows | Windows 10 Pro |\\n| Activation | Activated |\\n| Office | Microsoft 365 |\\n| Office Activation | Not activated |\\n| Word | Opens, cannot edit |\\n| Excel | Opens, cannot edit |\\n| Customer Data | D:\\\\Customer Records |\\n| Backup | Not verified |\\n| Antivirus | Defender |\\n| Other Apps | Tally |\\n| Main Issue | Office licensing |\\n| Data Risk | HIGH |\\n| Proposed Fix | Verify M365 license before repair |\\n| Status | Assessment only |\\n\\n### Important: first round = observation only\\n\\nFor your **first pass through all 10 PCs**, avoid:\\n\\n- Formatting\\n- Windows reinstall\\n- Office uninstall\\n- Deleting old user profiles\\n- Deleting folders\\n- Moving customer data\\n- Registry cleaning tools\\n- Pirated Office activators\\n- BIOS changes\\n- Large Windows feature upgrades\\n\\nYou can perform harmless tests, but collect the environment first.\\n\\nAfter all 10 PCs are assessed, we'll probably have something like:\\n\\n**PC01 – Office activation problem**  \\n**PC02 – unsupported Windows + old Office**  \\n**PC03 – Excel corruption**  \\n**PC04 – low disk space**  \\n**PC05 – healthy**  \\n**PC06 – Office installation damaged**  \\netc.\\n\\nThen we can group them and solve similar problems together instead of treating every machine differently.\\n\\nAnd when you reach the first system, just send me something like:\\n\\n\u003e PC1  \\n\u003e Windows: Windows 10 Pro  \\n\u003e RAM: 8GB  \\n\u003e CPU: i5  \\n\u003e Storage: 1TB HDD  \\n\u003e Office: Microsoft 365  \\n\u003e Activation: Unlicensed Product  \\n\u003e Word: opens but can't edit  \\n\u003e Excel: same  \\n\u003e Data: D drive customer folders  \\n\u003e Backup: unknown\\n\\nYou can Our first step is to protect the existing data, take a controlled backup with client approval, and only then perform software troubleshooting.”\\n\\nFor this client, follow a **data-first checklist**:\\n\\n1. **Take written approval before touching systems.** Mention that customer/business data is confidential and will not be accessed beyond what is needed for troubleshooting.\\n\\n2. **Do not use personal USB drives, WhatsApp, email, Google Drive, or any external cloud storage** for their files unless the client explicitly approves that method.\\n\\n3. **Create a backup before any risky action.** Prefer the client’s own external drive/NAS/server. Record:\\n   - PC name\\n   - backup location\\n   - folders backed up\\n   - backup date/time\\n   - approximate size\\n   - whether backup was verified\\n\\n4. **Do not format/reinstall Windows as the first solution.** Start with non-destructive fixes:\\n   - Office sign-in/license check\\n   - Office Quick Repair\\n   - Office Online Repair only after backup\\n   - Windows updates\\n   - file association fixes\\n   - disk-space cleanup\\n   - permissions/read-only checks\\n   - application repair\\n   - driver/network fixes\\n\\n5. **Before deleting anything, get approval.** Even temporary cleanup should avoid Documents/Desktop/customer folders. Never delete unknown folders just because they look old.\\n\\n6. **Do not open customer files unnecessarily.** For Word/Excel testing, create a **new test document** first. If an existing customer file must be opened, do it in front of the authorized employee wherever possible.\\n\\n7. **Keep a change log for every PC.** Example:\\n\\n| PC | Problem | Backup | Action | Data changed/deleted | Result |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| PC-01 | Word read-only | Verified | M365 activation fixed | None | Working |\\n| PC-02 | Excel not opening | Verified | Office repaired | None | Working |\\n| PC-03 | Windows 7 | Verified | No upgrade performed | None | Recommendation given |\\n\\n8. **For OS upgrade/reinstallation**, stop and get separate written approval because that is the highest-risk activity.\\n\\n9. **After every fix**, verify:\\n   - customer folders still exist\\n   - file count/important folders are accessible\\n   - Word/Excel opens\\n   - documents save correctly\\n   - business applications work\\n   - printer/network works if relevant\\n\\n10. **At the end, hand over a report stating “No customer data was copied outside client-controlled storage”** if that is factually true.\\n\\nThe key message to the client should be: **Nanobricks is not coming only to repair software; you are doing a controlled endpoint health and remediation activity with confidentiality and data preservation as primary controls.**\\n\\nI would also recommend you ask them to sign a simple **Service Authorization + Data Confidentiality Acknowledgement** before you start. That protects both the client and bro. Since it’s only **10 systems** and you also have to **identify + fix the issues**, handle it as a controlled onsite assessment and remediation activity.\\n\\nUse this exact checklist for Nanobricks.\\n\\n### Nanobricks – 10 System Assessment \u0026 Fix Checklist\\n\\n| Check | What to verify | Action |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| Asset details | User name, PC name, brand/model, serial no. | Record in sheet |\\n| CPU / RAM | Processor, RAM size | Check whether sufficient |\\n| Storage | HDD/SSD, free space, disk health | Upgrade/cleanup if required |\\n| Windows version | Windows 7/10/11, edition, build | Mark supported/unsupported |\\n| Windows activation | Activated / not activated | Report licensing issue |\\n| Windows updates | Pending/failed updates | Update after approval |\\n| Office version | Office 2010/2013/2016/2019/2021/M365 | Record exact version |\\n| Office activation | Licensed/activated? | Fix activation or flag license |\\n| Word | Opens? Can create/edit/save? | Repair/reinstall if needed |\\n| Excel | Opens? Can edit/save formulas? | Repair/reinstall if needed |\\n| PowerPoint | Open/create/save test | Repair if needed |\\n| Outlook | Opens/profile working? | Check if applicable |\\n| File association | DOCX/XLSX opening with correct app | Fix association |\\n| M365 login | Correct Microsoft account/license assigned | Sign in/verify |\\n| Browser | Chrome/Edge version | Update |\\n| Antivirus | Installed/running/up-to-date | Fix or recommend |\\n| Other applications | ERP/Tally/Java/PDF/etc. | Check compatibility |\\n| Startup apps | Unnecessary startup applications | Disable where safe |\\n| Temporary files | Excess temp/cache | Cleanup |\\n| Disk health | SMART/basic disk status | Flag failing drives |\\n| Device Manager | Missing drivers/errors | Install correct drivers |\\n| Internet/LAN | Internet, DNS, Wi-Fi/LAN | Troubleshoot |\\n| Printer/scanner | Device working and drivers | Fix if in scope |\\n| Backup | Important user data backed up | Mandatory before major changes |\\n| User data | Desktop/Documents/downloads etc. | Verify before reinstall |\\n| Admin rights | Local administrator availability | Confirm with client |\\n| Security | Unknown apps, cracks, malware indicators | Report/remove with approval |\\n\\nFor **Word/Excel problems**, specifically test each machine like this:\\n\\n**Test 1:** Open Word  \\n**Test 2:** Create a new document  \\n**Test 3:** Type something  \\n**Test 4:** Save locally  \\n**Test 5:** Close and reopen it  \\n**Test 6:** Open an existing `.docx`  \\n**Test 7:** Edit and save existing file\\n\\nDo the same for Excel.\\n\\nIf Word opens but says **“Unlicensed Product”, “Activation Required”, “Product Deactivated”**, etc., don't randomly reinstall first. Check:\\n\\n**File → Account → Product Information**\\n\\nRecord:\\n- Office edition\\n- Account signed in\\n- Activation status\\n- License/product details\\n\\nVery often, “Word opens but cannot edit” can be due to:\\n- Office not activated\\n- Microsoft 365 subscription expired\\n- User signed into wrong account\\n- File opened in Protected View\\n- File is read-only\\n- Office installation damaged\\n- Unsupported/very old Office version\\n- File permissions\\n- OneDrive/SharePoint access issue\\n\\nSo diagnose before reinstalling.\\n\\n### Use a simple status system\\n\\nFor each of the 10 systems mark:\\n\\n**GREEN – OK**  \\nEverything working and supported.\\n\\n**AMBER – Fix required**  \\nExample:\\n- Office repair\\n- Windows updates\\n- Driver issue\\n- Low storage\\n- M365 login problem\\n\\n**RED – Upgrade/replacement/licensing required**  \\nExample:\\n- Windows 7\\n- Very old Office\\n- HDD failure\\n- Unsupported hardware\\n- No valid Office license\\n\\nYour working Excel sheet can look like:\\n\\n| PC | User | CPU/RAM | Windows | Office | Activation | Word | Excel | Other Issues | Status | Action |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| PC01 | Accounts | i5/8GB | Win 11 | M365 | Active | OK | OK | None | Green | None |\\n| PC02 | HR | i3/4GB | Win 7 | Office 2010 | Active | Opens | Error | Old OS | Red | Upgrade |\\n| PC03 | Admin | i5/8GB | Win 10 | M365 | Not Active | Read only | Read only | License | Amber | Fix license |\\n\\nFor the visit itself, I would follow this order:\\n\\n**1. Take client approval and backup important files.**  \\n**2. Inventory all 10 PCs first.**  \\n**3. Don't immediately make changes while checking PC-1.** Finish the initial inventory so you understand the environment.  \\n**4. Classify supported/unsupported software and hardware.**  \\n**5. Diagnose Office/Windows issues.**  \\n**6. Fix low-risk items: activation/account, Office repair, updates, drivers, associations, storage cleanup, etc.**  \\n**7. For OS upgrades/reinstallation/hardware replacement, take approval before proceeding.**  \\n**8. Retest Word, Excel, business applications and printer/network after changes.**  \\n**9. Take user confirmation.**  \\n**10. Provide a final Nanobricks report.**\\n\\nAlso maintain two columns:\\n\\n**Before Status** → Word not editable  \\n**After Status** → Office license corrected, Word edit/save verified\\n\\nThat becomes proof of the work Nanobricks completed.\\n\\nOne important rule: **don't use cracked Windows/Office keys or unofficial activators.** If licensing is the problem, report it separately and give the company the proper Microsoft licensing option.\\n\\nFor 10 systems, the professional final deliverable should ideally be just **3 things**:\\n\\n1. **10-PC Asset \u0026 Software Inventory**\\n2. **Issue / Remediation Tracker**\\n3. **Final Standardization Recommendation**\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n\u003e **Recommended company standard:** Windows 11 Pro + Microsoft 365 + Edge/Chrome + approved PDF reader + antivirus/EDR + required business apps, with all endpoints patched and licensed.\\n\\nBut finalize that standard only after checking the hardware and their business applications.\",{},{\"_180\":181,\"_182\":-5,\"_108\":354},1790482517.942294,{\"_175\":201,\"_101\":265},{\"_188\":189,\"_190\":107,\"_192\":350,\"_194\":351,\"_196\":197,\"_128\":129,\"_130\":32,\"_131\":335,\"_133\":134,\"_135\":336,\"_137\":336,\"_123\":39,\"_138\":39,\"_38\":39,\"_139\":140,\"_141\":32,\"_142\":352,\"_199\":105,\"_144\":353},1790482516.0231493,1790482517.935194,\"61c9dfab-2399-4394-86e6-3f93eaa8387f\",[],{},\"98bf97f4-88f1-41e4-bb18-c1c85b4d7a83\",{\"_180\":181,\"_182\":-5,\"_108\":363},1790482516.275196,{\"_175\":214,\"_215\":216,\"_217\":-5,\"_218\":-5,\"_219\":-5},{\"_123\":39,\"_126\":360,\"_209\":107,\"_210\":211,\"_192\":350,\"_128\":129,\"_130\":105,\"_131\":335,\"_133\":134,\"_135\":336,\"_137\":336,\"_138\":39,\"_38\":39,\"_139\":140,\"_141\":32,\"_142\":361,\"_144\":362},[],\"3849c7da-9a25-4dd7-83dc-f41a43f37b5b\",[],{},\"83bec560-1c0b-4740-ad74-82982583c018\",{\"_180\":233,\"_182\":-5,\"_108\":373},1790482515.347341,1790482549.410418,{\"_175\":176,\"_177\":371},{\"_273\":32,\"_274\":32,\"_131\":335,\"_227\":-5,\"_135\":336,\"_137\":336,\"_228\":370,\"_141\":32},{\"_181\":230},[372],\"Some This applies to the following editions: Enterprise, Enterprise N, Home Basic, Home Premium, Home Premium N, Professional, Professional for Em...\",{\"_448\":160,\"_449\":450,\"_451\":191},\"https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/lifecycle/products/windows-7?utm_source=chatgpt.com\",{\"_448\":160,\"_449\":450,\"_451\":301},{\"_156\":655,\"_158\":-5,\"_159\":656,\"_161\":657,\"_34\":658,\"_164\":659,\"_166\":660,\"_167\":-5,\"_168\":627,\"_628\":661,\"_630\":-5,\"_12\":631,\"_632\":-5,\"_633\":-5},{\"_156\":644,\"_158\":-5,\"_159\":645,\"_161\":646,\"_34\":647,\"_164\":648,\"_166\":649,\"_167\":-5,\"_168\":627,\"_628\":650,\"_630\":-5,\"_12\":631,\"_632\":-5,\"_633\":-5},{\"_156\":621,\"_158\":-5,\"_159\":622,\"_161\":623,\"_34\":624,\"_164\":625,\"_166\":626,\"_167\":-5,\"_168\":627,\"_628\":629,\"_630\":-5,\"_12\":631,\"_632\":-5,\"_633\":-5},{\"_156\":617,\"_158\":-5,\"_159\":618,\"_161\":606,\"_34\":619,\"_164\":620,\"_166\":-5,\"_167\":-5,\"_168\":169,\"_170\":32},{\"_156\":605,\"_158\":-5,\"_159\":606,\"_161\":607,\"_34\":608,\"_164\":609,\"_166\":216,\"_167\":-5,\"_168\":610,\"_611\":612,\"_613\":32},\" \",6779,6780,[],[],\"sources_footnote\",\"sources\",[614],\"has_images\",{\"_17\":615,\"_439\":399,\"_447\":616},\"FAQ about the end of support for Windows 7 | Microsoft Learn\",\"Microsoft Learn\",\"memcite\",6770,[],[],\"citeturn506697search7\",4560,4584,[396,397],[],\"([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-apps/deploy/assess-microsoft-365-apps?utm_source=chatgpt.com))\",\"grouped_webpages\",\"items\",[634],\"fallback_items\",\"done\",\"error\",\"style\",{\"_17\":635,\"_439\":397,\"_447\":616,\"_446\":-5,\"_442\":216,\"_636\":-5,\"_637\":638,\"_164\":639,\"_640\":-5,\"_641\":-5},\"Assess your environment and requirements for deploying Microsoft 365 Apps - Microsoft 365 Apps | Microsoft Learn\",\"attribution_segments\",\"supporting_websites\",[],[642],\"hue\",\"attributions\",{\"_448\":160,\"_449\":450,\"_451\":643},7,\"citeturn506697search0\",3506,3530,[394,395],[],\"([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/lifecycle/products/windows-server-2019?utm_source=chatgpt.com))\",[651],{\"_17\":497,\"_439\":395,\"_447\":616,\"_446\":-5,\"_442\":216,\"_636\":-5,\"_637\":652,\"_164\":653,\"_640\":-5,\"_641\":-5},[],[654],{\"_448\":160,\"_449\":450,\"_451\":160},\"citeturn506697search1turn506697search4\",3292,3334,[392,393,398,399],[],\"([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/troubleshoot/windows-client/windows-7-eos-faq/windows-7-end-support-faq-general?utm_source=chatgpt.com))\",[662],{\"_17\":615,\"_439\":399,\"_447\":616,\"_446\":-5,\"_442\":216,\"_636\":-5,\"_637\":663,\"_164\":664,\"_640\":-5,\"_641\":-5},[667],[665,666],{\"_448\":160,\"_449\":450,\"_451\":107},{\"_448\":160,\"_449\":450,\"_451\":593},{\"_17\":590,\"_439\":393,\"_446\":-5,\"_442\":216,\"_447\":616},[174],[670],\"Bro, what they are asking for sounds like an **IT Software Inventory \u0026 Standardization Assessment**.\\n\\nThey probably have different systems with things like Windows 7, Windows 10/11, maybe Windows Server 2019, different Office versions, Microsoft 365 web apps, locally installed Word/Excel, and other applications. They want you to first **identify what exists, find differences, and recommend one standard configuration**.\\n\\nFor Nanobricks, handle it professionally like a small consulting engagement—not like “I’ll come and check the PCs.”\\n\\n### How you should approach the client\\n\\n**Phase 1 — Requirement discussion**\\n\\nFirst meet their IT/admin/contact person and confirm:\\n- Number of laptops/desktops/servers\\n- Approximate number of users\\n- Office locations\\n- Domain/Active Directory or workgroup setup\\n- Whether devices are company-owned\\n- Microsoft licensing they currently have\\n- Whether they want only an **assessment** or also **upgrade + implementation**\\n- Critical applications they cannot disturb\\n\\nSay something like:\\n\\n\u003e “Nanobricks will first conduct an IT software inventory and standardization assessment. We’ll document the operating systems, Microsoft Office/Microsoft 365 versions, installed business applications, licensing status and unsupported software. Based on that, we’ll provide a gap analysis and recommended standard configuration.”\\n\\nThat immediately makes the engagement sound structured.\\n\\n### Phase 2 — Take an inventory\\n\\nFor every computer, create one row in your assessment sheet.\\n\\n| Asset | User | OS | OS Build | Office | M365 | Browser | Business Apps | Antivirus/EDR | License | Status |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| PC-001 | Accounts | Windows 7 Pro | SP1 | Office 2013 | No | Chrome | Tally | XYZ AV | Check | Upgrade |\\n| PC-002 | HR | Windows 11 Pro | 24H2 | M365 Apps | Yes | Edge | HR App | Defender | Licensed | Standard |\\n| PC-003 | Manager | Windows 10 | … | Office 2019 | Yes | Chrome | ERP Client | Defender | Check | Review |\\n\\nAlso collect:\\n- CPU\\n- RAM\\n- Disk type/capacity\\n- 32-bit / 64-bit architecture\\n- Computer name\\n- IP/MAC only if necessary\\n- Serial/service tag\\n- Windows activation\\n- Office activation\\n- Installed application names + versions\\n- Any old Java/.NET/runtime dependencies\\n- Printer/scanner software if business-critical\\n\\nDo **not collect user passwords**.\\n\\n### Phase 3 — Determine the target standard\\n\\nThis is probably what they mean by making everything “same/equal.”\\n\\nYou could propose something like:\\n\\n**Nanobricks Recommended Endpoint Standard**\\n\\n\u003e Windows 11 Pro 64-bit  \\n\u003e Microsoft 365 Apps for Business/Enterprise  \\n\u003e Word / Excel / PowerPoint / Outlook – consistent release channel  \\n\u003e Edge + Chrome latest supported version  \\n\u003e Microsoft Defender or client's approved endpoint protection  \\n\u003e One approved PDF reader  \\n\u003e Approved business applications only  \\n\u003e Standard patching policy  \\n\u003e Standard local administrator policy  \\n\u003e Standard browser configuration\\n\\nBut you should select the final standard only after checking hardware compatibility and business application dependencies.\\n\\nOne particularly important issue: **Windows 7 should be highlighted immediately as unsupported**. Microsoft ended Windows 7 support on January 14, 2020, and Microsoft 365 Apps are no longer supported on Windows 7. citeturn506697search1turn506697search4\\n\\nIf by “19” they mean **Windows Server 2019**, don't confuse it with desktop Windows. Windows Server 2019 remains under extended Microsoft support until January 10, 2029. citeturn506697search0\\n\\n### Phase 4 — Perform gap analysis\\n\\nYour deliverable should not just say “PC 1 has Windows 7.”\\n\\nClassify each machine:\\n\\n**Green — Compliant**\\n\u003e Already matches proposed standard.\\n\\n**Amber — Remediation required**\\n\u003e Example: Windows 11 but old Office / missing updates / wrong applications.\\n\\n**Red — Upgrade or replacement**\\n\u003e Example: Windows 7, unsupported Office, unlicensed software, incompatible hardware.\\n\\nThen report something like:\\n\\n\u003e Total endpoints assessed: 42  \\n\u003e Standard compliant: 21  \\n\u003e Software remediation required: 13  \\n\u003e OS upgrade required: 5  \\n\u003e Hardware replacement recommended: 3  \\n\u003e Office/M365 licensing review: 9\\n\\nThat is much more valuable to a client than simply giving them an Excel list.\\n\\nMicrosoft itself recommends assessing device platform, operating system, 32/64-bit architecture, existing Office versions, licensing/accounts, network capability and application compatibility before a Microsoft 365 Apps deployment, so this approach closely follows Microsoft's enterprise assessment guidance. citeturn506697search7\\n\\n### Phase 5 — Give them two separate commercial options\\n\\nDon't bundle everything into one vague charge.\\n\\nYou can quote:\\n\\n**Option A – Assessment only**\\n\\nNanobricks performs:\\n- Device inventory\\n- OS/software inventory\\n- Licensing observations\\n- Unsupported software identification\\n- Gap analysis\\n- Standardization recommendation\\n- Final assessment report\\n\\nThen the customer decides whether to implement.\\n\\n**Option B – Assessment + remediation**\\n\\nEverything above plus:\\n- Windows upgrades\\n- Office/M365 deployment\\n- Application installation/removal\\n- Standard configuration\\n- Patch/update configuration\\n- Migration assistance\\n- Validation/testing\\n- Final handover report\\n\\nAnd later you can convert this client into an **AMC / Managed IT Support customer**:\\n\\n\u003e Monthly endpoint monitoring  \\n\u003e Windows patch management  \\n\u003e Software inventory  \\n\u003e License management  \\n\u003e Microsoft 365 administration  \\n\u003e Endpoint security  \\n\u003e User onboarding/offboarding  \\n\u003e Backup checks  \\n\u003e Remote support\\n\\nThat is where this small requirement can become a recurring Nanobricks customer.\\n\\n### One important thing for your first visit\\n\\nDon't immediately install software or upgrade anything.\\n\\nFirst say:\\n\\n\u003e **Day 1 / Discovery:** assessment only.  \\n\u003e **After report approval:** remediation/change activity.\\n\\nEspecially with Windows 7 or old machines, there may be legacy accounting, manufacturing or ERP software that only works on that OS. Upgrading without compatibility testing could interrupt their business.\\n\\nBefore any upgrade, get:\\n- Client approval\\n- Data backup confirmation\\n- Application compatibility confirmation\\n- Maintenance window\\n- Rollback plan\\n\\n### What I'd call the service\\n\\nFor Nanobricks, use:\\n\\n**IT Infrastructure \u0026 Software Standardization Assessment**\\n\\nor\\n\\n**Endpoint Software Inventory \u0026 Standardization Assessment**\\n\\nThe second name matches this requirement very well.\\n\\nAnd bro, this is actually a **good first corporate service for Nanobricks** because you don't need to sell them a massive cloud project immediately. Start with 20–50 endpoints, demonstrate professionalism, give them a useful report, then expand into Microsoft 365, security, licensing, cloud, AMC and support.
